From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

President of Argentina since 2023

"Milei" redirects here. For his sister, see [Karina Milei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karina_Milei "Karina Milei"). For the river, see [Milei (river)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milei_(river) "Milei (river)").

In this [Argentine name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_name "Argentine name"), the [surname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surname "Surname") is _Milei_.

**Javier Gerardo Milei**[\[a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-1) (born 22 October 1970) is an Argentine politician and economist who has served as the [president of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Argentina "President of Argentina") since 2023. He served as a member of the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Chamber_of_Deputies "Argentine Chamber of Deputies") from 2021 to 2023.

Born in [Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenos_Aires "Buenos Aires"), Milei attended the [University of Belgrano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Belgrano "University of Belgrano"), where he obtained a degree in economics, and undertook postgraduate coursework at the Instituto de Desarrollo Económico y Social,[\[1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-2) and at [Torcuato di Tella University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torcuato_di_Tella_University "Torcuato di Tella University"),[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-3) though neither resulted in a completed degree.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-4) He rose to public prominence in the 2010s by appearing as a [pundit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pundit "Pundit") on various television shows in which he was a vocal critic of the Argentine political establishment.

In the [2021 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Argentine_legislative_election "2021 Argentine legislative election"), Milei was elected to the [Argentine Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Chamber_of_Deputies "Argentine Chamber of Deputies"), representing the City of Buenos Aires for [La Libertad Avanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Libertad_Avanza "La Libertad Avanza"). As a national deputy, he limited his legislative activities to voting, focusing instead on critiquing Argentina's political elite and its propensity for high government spending. Milei pledged not to raise taxes and donated his national deputy salary through a monthly raffle. He defeated the incumbent economy minister [Sergio Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Massa "Sergio Massa") in the second round of the [2023 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Argentine_general_election "2023 Argentine general election"), on a platform that held the ideological dominance of [Kirchnerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirchnerism "Kirchnerism") responsible for the ongoing [Argentine monetary crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%93present_Argentine_monetary_crisis "2018–present Argentine monetary crisis").

Milei is known for his flamboyant personality, distinctive personal style, and strong media presence, including his catchphrase " _[¡Viva la libertad, carajo!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C2%A1Viva_la_libertad,_carajo! "¡Viva la libertad, carajo!")_".[\[b\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-VLLC-5) He has been described politically as a [right-wing populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_populism "Right-wing populism") and [libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-libertarianism "Right-libertarianism") who supports _[laissez-faire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laissez-faire "Laissez-faire")_ economics, aligning specifically with [minarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night-watchman_state "Night-watchman state") and [anarcho-capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-capitalism "Anarcho-capitalism") principles. He has proposed a comprehensive overhaul of the country's fiscal and structural policies. On social issues, he opposes [abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_in_Argentina "Abortion in Argentina") and [euthanasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_euthanasia#Argentina "Legality of euthanasia"), and he supports civilian [ownership of firearms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overview_of_gun_laws_by_nation#Argentina "Overview of gun laws by nation"). He also supports " [freedom of choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_choice "Freedom of choice")" on drug policy. On [foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Argentina "Foreign relations of Argentina"), he promotes [Atlanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanticism "Atlanticism"), advocating closer relations within the [Western world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_world "Western world"), especially with both [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Israel_relations "Argentina–Israel relations") and the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93United_States_relations "Argentina–United States relations") (mainly under the [Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump "Second presidency of Donald Trump")), while shifting Argentina away from [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS").

Since taking office, Milei has focused on stabilizing Argentina's economy through spending cuts, deregulation, and monetary restraint after decades of economic mismanagement. According to [National Institute of Statistics and Censuses of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_and_Censuses_of_Argentina "National Institute of Statistics and Censuses of Argentina") (INDEC), inflation fell sharply, the government achieved its first fiscal surplus since 2008, and poverty declined to an estimated 31.6% in early 2025, while wages partially recovered despite uneven sector performance. Economic growth modestly resumed, though rising living and healthcare costs and reduced consumption continued to strain public support. Milei's reforms were reinforced by La Libertad Avanza winning a plurality in the [2025 legislative elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Argentine_legislative_election "2025 Argentine legislative election").

## Early life and education

Javier Gerardo Milei was born on 22 October 1970 in [Palermo, Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palermo,_Buenos_Aires "Palermo, Buenos Aires"), to Norberto Milei and Alicia Lucich.[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6)[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-7)[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-8) He grew up in the neighborhood of [Villa Devoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_Devoto "Villa Devoto") and later moved to the [Sáenz Peña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A1enz_Pe%C3%B1a,_Buenos_Aires "Sáenz Peña, Buenos Aires") district.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) Alicia worked as a [homemaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homemaking "Homemaking"),[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Criales_2023-10) and Norberto was a [bus driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_driver "Bus driver")[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Compte_2018-11)[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-12) who later became a successful businessman.[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-13)

Milei is of paternal [Italian descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Argentines "Italian Argentines"). His grandfather came from [Rossano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rossano "Rossano") in the [Calabria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calabria "Calabria") region of [Southern Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Italy "Southern Italy") in 1926.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-14)[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-15) His father's maternal great-grandparents came from the municipalities [Francavilla in Sinni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francavilla_in_Sinni "Francavilla in Sinni") and [Pignola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pignola "Pignola") in the [Basilicata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilicata "Basilicata") region of Southern Italy. On his maternal side, he is of [Croatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Argentines "Croatian Argentines") and Italian descent. His mother's paternal grandparents and great-grandparents came from [Jelsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelsa,_Croatia "Jelsa, Croatia") on the island of [Hvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hvar "Hvar") and settled in [San Lorenzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Lorenzo_Department "San Lorenzo Department"), a department in the province of [Santa Fe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_Province "Santa Fe Province") in Argentina.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-16) His mother's maternal grandparents came from [San Severino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Severino_Marche "San Severino Marche") in the [Marche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marche "Marche") region of [Central Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Italy "Central Italy") and settled also in San Lorenzo.[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-17) Milei revealed in 2024 that his grandfather, a great influence in his life, discovered that he [was Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who_is_a_Jew%3F "Who is a Jew?") from [matrilineal descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matrilineality_in_Judaism "Matrilineality in Judaism") shortly before his death. He expressed that his grandfather's maternal grandfather may have been a [rabbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbi "Rabbi").[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:2-18)

Milei says he was subject to [abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abuse "Child abuse") during his childhood, and after he moved away from home as a young adult, he did not speak to his parents for a decade.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Pasquini_2023-19)[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Criales_2023-10) By 2018, he had stopped talking to them entirely and regarded them as dead.[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-perfil-20) Milei has said the abuse he suffered contributed to his ability to handle adversity.[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-21) He was supported by his maternal grandmother and his younger sister, [Karina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karina_Milei "Karina Milei"),[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6) with whom he shares a close bond[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-22) and whom he calls "the boss".[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-23) Milei attended [Catholic schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_school "Catholic school"),[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6) including the Cardenal Copello secondary school.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) At school, he was nicknamed _el Loco_ ("the Madman") for his outbursts and passionate rhetoric.[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6) In his late teens and early adulthood, Milei sang in the [cover band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cover_band "Cover band") Everest, which mostly played [Rolling Stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stones "Rolling Stones") covers. He played [goalkeeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goalkeeper_(association_football) "Goalkeeper (association football)") for the [Chacarita Juniors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chacarita_Juniors "Chacarita Juniors") football team until 1989,[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Compte_2018-11)[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-24) when Argentina suffered from [hyperinflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_hyperinflation_in_Argentina "1989 hyperinflation in Argentina"), and he committed to a career in economics.[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-perfil-20)

The collapse of [Argentina's exchange rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_exchange_rates_of_Argentine_currency "Historical exchange rates of Argentine currency") led to Milei's interest in economics in the early 1980s.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9)[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Compte_2018-11) Milei studied introductory economics and the law of [supply and demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supply_and_demand "Supply and demand"), which seemed to him at odds with the ongoing hyperinflation; he said he saw people "throwing themselves on top of the merchandise" in a supermarket and began to study economics in more detail to understand it.[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-25) For 15 years,\[ _[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\] he worked at the private company Corporación América as the chief economist and financial adviser to [Eduardo Eurnekian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_Eurnekian "Eduardo Eurnekian").[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-26)

Milei is the author of several books,[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-27) including _[El camino del libertario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_camino_del_libertario "El camino del libertario")_.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Chavez_2022-28) He has a notable presence on television, with a 2018 ranking by Ejes showing him as the most interviewed economist on TV, at 235 interviews totaling 193,347 seconds.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-29) Milei also hosted his own radio show, _Demoliendo mitos_ ( _Demolishing Myths_),[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Infobae_2018-30) featuring regular appearances by [Alberdian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Bautista_Alberdi "Juan Bautista Alberdi") and [right-wing libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_libertarian "Right-wing libertarian") personalities, including economist and businessman Gustavo Lazzari, lawyer Pablo Torres Barthe, and political scientist María Zaldívar.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-31)[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-32)

## Early political career

### Rise to prominence

During the 2010s, Milei regularly participated in televised debates, where he was known for using strong language and confrontational rhetoric. In some instances, he insulted other participants and used profanity when expressing his political views, including during a debate with Buenos Aires chief of government [Horacio Rodríguez Larreta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horacio_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Larreta "Horacio Rodríguez Larreta").[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-33)[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-34)[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-35)[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-36)[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-37)[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-38)[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-39)[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-40)[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-41) This led many commentators to label him anti-political or disruptive.[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-BBC_News_Mundo_2023-42) United States Senator [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz "Ted Cruz") jokingly proposed inviting him to the [2024 Republican Party presidential debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_Party_presidential_debates_and_forums "2024 Republican Party presidential debates and forums").[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Infobae_2023-43)\[ _[importance?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:What_Wikipedia_is_not#Encyclopedic_content "Wikipedia:What Wikipedia is not")_\]

In 2017, Milei praised former economy minister [Domingo Cavallo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domingo_Cavallo "Domingo Cavallo"), describing him as the best to have held the position in Argentina. He credited Cavallo with ending the 1989 hyperinflation and initiating state reforms and attributed the 2001 economic crisis to earlier policy decisions that Cavallo was unable to reverse in time.[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-44) In November 2017, Milei drew attention for criticizing the Faculty of Economic Sciences at the University of Buenos Aires, which he described as a center of [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism "Marxism") indoctrination. He argued that this had contributed to what he called a widespread dominance of [Keynesian economic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keynesian_economics "Keynesian economics") thought in Argentina.[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-45)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Javier_Milei_en_una_entrevista_de_TN.png/250px-Javier_Milei_en_una_entrevista_de_TN.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Javier_Milei_en_una_entrevista_de_TN.png) Milei during an interview on _[Todo Noticias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todo_Noticias "Todo Noticias")_ in 2019

In June 2018, Milei was involved in a public dispute with journalist Teresita Frías after he called her “dumb” in response to calling his political views [totalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Totalitarianism "Totalitarianism").[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-46)[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-47) Consequently, a local court briefly imposed appearance restrictions on Milei, though no charges were filed.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-48)[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-49) That same year, Milei made his acting debut in the play _El consultorio de Milei_, alongside Claudio Rico and Diego Sucalesca. In 2019, [Todo Noticias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todo_Noticias "Todo Noticias") included him on its list of the most influential people in Argentina. In 2020, he publicly supported protests against the government of President [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez "Alberto Fernández").[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9)

### 2021 legislative campaign

From 2020 to 2021, Milei was a member of [Avanza Libertad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avanza_Libertad "Avanza Libertad") (Advance, Freedom), a political party founded by [José Luis Espert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Luis_Espert "José Luis Espert").[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-50) During his campaign for the [Argentine Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Chamber_of_Deputies "Argentine Chamber of Deputies"), Milei focused on Buenos Aires neighborhoods, where he took strolls and had talks with ordinary people.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-51) He pledged not to support any tax increases or new taxes.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-IP_Noticias_2021-52) He ran under the slogan "I didn't come here to lead lambs, but to awaken lions", denouncing what he saw as a political caste,[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-53)[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-54)[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-55) which he said was composed of "useless, parasitic politicians who have never worked".[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-56) He called politicians "rats" and said they formed "a parasitic caste" that thinks only about getting rich.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Molina_2023-57) He used phrases like "I'm here to kick these criminals out"[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6)[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Alonso_2023-58) and was especially supported by youth;[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-59) he promoted his political views on television, radio, and [YouTube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube "YouTube").[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) Additionally, Milei reconciled with his parents.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-60)

In July 2021, Milei established the coalition [La Libertad Avanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Libertad_Avanza "La Libertad Avanza") (Freedom Advances),[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-61)[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-62) which secured third place in [primary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_election "Primary election") with 13.66 percent of the vote and third in the [2021 Argentine legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Argentine_legislative_election "2021 Argentine legislative election") with 17 percent,[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-63) and the [libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarianism "Libertarianism") coalition entered the [Argentine Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_National_Congress "Argentine National Congress").[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-64)[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-El_Diario_2021-65)[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-66) They performed best in [Córdoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%B3rdoba,_Argentina "Córdoba, Argentina") and [Santa Fe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe,_Argentina "Santa Fe, Argentina"), the second- and third-most populated districts in the country. They performed well in [Peronist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peronism "Peronism") strongholds in [North Tucumán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucum%C3%A1n_Province "Tucumán Province"), [Salta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salta_Province "Salta Province"), [La Rioja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Rioja_Province,_Argentina "La Rioja Province, Argentina"), [San Juan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan_Province,_Argentina "San Juan Province, Argentina"), and [Santa Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Cruz_Province,_Argentina "Santa Cruz Province, Argentina") in Patagonia, which is considered the cradle of [Kirchnerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirchnerism "Kirchnerism").[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9)

## National deputyship

After being elected to the Chamber of Deputies, Javier Milei began raffling off his monthly salary, stating that the initiative aimed to “return money to the citizens” and criticizing taxation as coercive. He described the salary as “dirty money” taken by force and claimed he was returning funds allegedly “stolen by the political caste”. The raffles, open to the general public, had distributed over seven million pesos by the time of reporting.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-67)[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-68)[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Il_Post_2023-6)[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Nunes_2023-69)[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:0-70)

As a deputy, Milei had an attendance rate of 52% in the chamber as of April 2023.[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-71) By August 2023, he had not introduced any legislation or joined parliamentary commissions.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:6-72)[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:0-70) One of his absences was criticized by the opposition coalition Juntos por el Cambio, as it allowed the government to pass a tax increase on airline tickets by a single vote.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:6-72)[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-73)[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-74)

In July 2023, Milei was investigated over allegations of selling candidacies within La Libertad Avanza.[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-75)[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-76) Businessman [Juan Carlos Blumberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Carlos_Blumberg "Juan Carlos Blumberg") accused the coalition of turning politics into a business, a claim Milei denied. He was also accused of receiving financial and logistical support from Peronist provincial governments. Journalist Juan Luis González alleged that Milei accepted funding and aid from the Peronist sectors he publicly opposed.[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-iProfesional_2023-77) Prosecutor Ramiro González stated there was no concrete evidence to support the claims at the time. Milei dismissed the investigation as a political attack and called for an inquiry into the prosecutor's conduct, accusing him of harming his reputation.[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-78)[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-79) The case was closed in March 2024 due to a lack of sufficient evidence for formal charges.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-80)

### Coinx World promotion

On 18 December 2021, Milei promoted the financial services company Coinx World on his Instagram account after visiting its offices in Buenos Aires. In his post, he praised the company's investment platform and encouraged his followers to contact them. Coinx World used the visit for promotional purposes on social media, presenting itself as a [financial technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_technology "Financial technology") firm specializing in [algorithmic trading](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algorithmic_trading "Algorithmic trading"). The company stated that its meeting with Milei was a consultation on communication strategy and claimed that its operations were supported by El Salvadoran legislation, where it was headquartered.[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-81)[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Coinx_World-82)[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Coinx_Clarin-83)

Financial experts warned that CoinX's claims were unsustainable and compared its operation to a [Ponzi scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ponzi_scheme "Ponzi scheme"). At that time, Milei was not registered in Argentina's National Securities Commission to make invitations to the public to invest, nor did Coinx World have the authorization to operate in the public offering, which led the National Securities Commission to declare it as an irregular public offering in 2022.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-84)

In an interview with journalist Ernesto Tenembaum in June 2022, Milei claimed that he was paid for that public support just like he is paid for "all his opinions," and that the company "did not scam anyone". He explained that Coinx World "took money and used it to buy securities in US dollars. With the devaluation that occurred, they were able to pay significantly higher interest rates in Argentine pesos than those offered by Argentina's Central Bank, which allowed them to secure funding to invest in their crypto farms where they generated cryptocurrencies and had more money. The business model was well-structured." Milei could not explain why he encouraged his followers to invest in Coinx World and did not apologize to the victims when asked to do so.[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-85)[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Revista_Noticias-86)

Milei defended the company on social media when some followers pointed out it could be a [pyramid scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_scheme "Pyramid scheme"). "Coinx paid everyone", he said. "Do not let yourselves be fooled by the paid hitmen of Juntos por el Cambio."[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Revista_Noticias-86) In 2023, Coinx World was shut down by regulators after a criminal investigation for allegedly operating as a Ponzi scheme. Police raids were conducted on its offices in Buenos Aires and Santa Fe with four employees, including its CEO, being arrested. Investors, the majority of whom followed Milei on social media, sued him for losses estimated between [AR$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_peso "Argentine peso") 30,000,000 and [AR$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_peso "Argentine peso") 40,000,000 (approximately [US$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_dollar "United States dollar") 300,000 at the time). According to its website, Coinx World operated in Argentina, Brazil, and El Salvador.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Coinx_World-82)[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-87)

## Elections

### 2023 presidential campaign

Main article: [Javier Milei 2023 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei_2023_presidential_campaign "Javier Milei 2023 presidential campaign")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Javier_Milei_VIVA22_%283%29.jpg/250px-Javier_Milei_VIVA22_%283%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Javier_Milei_VIVA22_(3).jpg) Milei posing with [Villarruel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Villarruel "Victoria Villarruel") and [Santiago Abascal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Abascal "Santiago Abascal") in October 2022

As a member of the [Libertarian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_Party_(Argentina) "Libertarian Party (Argentina)"), Milei ran for [president of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Argentina "President of Argentina") as part of [La Libertad Avanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Libertad_Avanza "La Libertad Avanza"), a [right-wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_politics "Right-wing politics") political coalition. His running mate was [Victoria Villarruel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Villarruel "Victoria Villarruel").[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-88)[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-89) His younger sister Karina managed his campaign.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) In May 2022, Milei was rising in the polls.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-90) In June 2022, he officially launched his presidential campaign.[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-91) In March 2023, a poll showed that 17 percent of Argentines would vote for him and that his political coalition would become the third parliamentary force in the Argentine Congress. His rhetoric was attractive to under-30 voters born during the [1998–2002 Argentine great depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%932002_Argentine_great_depression "1998–2002 Argentine great depression") and facing the still ongoing [economic stagnation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_stagnation "Economic stagnation"). His supporters include those who once voted for Kirchnerism but would now vote for Milei as a protest even if they did not support his economic ideas.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Molina_2023-57) Lucas Romero, the head of Synopsis, a local political consulting firm and Andrei Roman, CEO of Brazil-based pollster Atlas Intel believe that Milei's victory in the 2023 elections was not necessarily a result of popular approval of his ideas but rather stemmed from widespread dissatisfaction with the political establishment, which had been strongly left-wing in its economic approach. The people thus wanted change.[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-92)[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-93)[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-94)[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-95)

As annual inflation rose above 100 percent in May 2023,[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Santamaria_2023-96) Milei's position in the polls rose.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-97) In June 2023, the markets welcomed [Sergio Massa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Massa "Sergio Massa")'s presidential candidacy, as it polarized the election between the ruling party and [Juntos por el Cambio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juntos_por_el_Cambio "Juntos por el Cambio"), reducing the "Milei factor".[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-98) Notable moments in Milei's campaign included a viral video of him tearing cards from a wallboard with the names of ministries that he wants to abolish and tossing them into the air as he said _afuera_ ("out"),[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-99) wielding a chainsaw on stage,[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Centenera_2023-100) smashing a piñata on air to symbolize his plans, calling [Pope Francis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Francis "Pope Francis") "a filthy leftist", and praising American gangster [Al Capone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Capone "Al Capone") as "a hero".[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-De_Guzman_2023-101)

### Primary and general elections

Further information: [2023 Argentine general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Argentine_general_election "2023 Argentine general election") and [2023 Argentine primary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Argentine_primary_elections "2023 Argentine primary elections")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/2023_Argentina_General_Election_map.svg/250px-2023_Argentina_General_Election_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2023_Argentina_General_Election_map.svg)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/2023_Argentina_Presidential_Election_-_Second_Round_Results.svg/250px-2023_Argentina_Presidential_Election_-_Second_Round_Results.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2023_Argentina_Presidential_Election_-_Second_Round_Results.svg)

First and second round results of the [2023 Argentine presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Argentine_general_election "2023 Argentine general election")

In the August 2023 primary elections, which are seen as an indication of how citizens are likely to vote in the October 2023 general election,[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-102) Milei emerged as the leading candidate,[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Reuters_2023-103)[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Al_Jazeera_2023-104)[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Go%C3%B1i_2023-105) with 30 percent of the vote, ahead of the traditional Peronism–Kirchnerism and [Macrism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Mauricio_Macri "Presidency of Mauricio Macri") that dominated the country in the 2010s.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) Milei's victory was celebrated by right-wing figures, including [Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro "Jair Bolsonaro"),[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Go%C3%B1i_2023-105) [José Antonio Kast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Antonio_Kast "José Antonio Kast"),[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Go%C3%B1i_2023-105) [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz "Ted Cruz"),[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Infobae_2023-43) and the Spanish conservative[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-The_European_Conservative-106) political party [Vox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vox_(political_party) "Vox (political party)").[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-SWI_swissinfo.ch_2023-107) Polls had predicted that Massa would secure the most votes as a candidate in the primaries, with Juntos por el Cambio expected to be the most supported coalition overall;[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-The_Economist_2023-108) Milei polled at about 20 percent[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Viriglio_2023-9) and was seen as an outsider candidate.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-109)[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-110) Initially, for the first round of the general election, with the possibility of a runoff in November,[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-111) Peronists saw Milei as a possible ally who would divide the votes of the _Juntos por el Cambio_ coalition.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-112)

As a result of his strong performance in the primaries, Milei was considered the front-runner in the general election. His rise has been placed within the context of the last two presidencies of Macri and Fernández. On 22 October, Milei advanced to the runoff, facing Massa[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-113) in what polls showed as a tight race.[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-114) In the runoff on 19 November, Milei defeated Massa in a landslide[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-115)[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-116)[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-117) and a historic election.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-118) It was the highest vote percentage since [Argentina's transition to democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina's_transition_to_democracy "Argentina's transition to democracy"). Observers generally saw Milei's win as a sign more of discontent with the _status quo_ than support for his politics,[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-De_Guzman_2023-101) and his victory was likened to [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump")'s in the United States and [Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro "Jair Bolsonaro")'s in Brazil.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-119)[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-120) Within the Argentine Congress, Milei's coalition achieved about 20% in the Chamber of Deputies and 10% in the Senate.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-121) In his victory speech, Milei pledged a new political era,[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-122)[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Bianchi,_Elliot_&_Misculin_2023-123) vowing to begin "the reconstruction of Argentina" and end its economic decline.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Bianchi,_Elliot_&_Misculin_2023-123)

### 2025 Argentine legislative elections

In the [2025 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Argentine_legislative_election "2025 Argentine legislative election"), La Libertad Avanza took first place with 40.84% of the vote, much stronger than expected,[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto8-124) and the opposition Peronist coalition [Homeland Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_Force "Homeland Force") took second place with 31.63% of the vote.[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-125)[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-126) Turnout was lower than usual, at 67.85 percent of the 36million eligible voters – the lowest tally for a national election since the return of democracy [in 1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Argentine_general_election "1983 Argentine general election"). Prior polling forecasted La Libertad Avanza attaining 30 to 35% of the vote.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-127) Several American media outlets reported the strong win as a renewed mandate for Milei's policies.[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-128)[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-129)[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto8-124)

## Presidency (2023–present)

Main article: [Presidency of Javier Milei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Javier_Milei "Presidency of Javier Milei")

See also: [History of Argentina §Milei administration (2023-present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Argentina#Milei_administration_(2023-present) "History of Argentina")

### Early actions (2023–2025)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Javier_Milei_y_Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez1.jpg/250px-Javier_Milei_y_Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Javier_Milei_y_Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez1.jpg) [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez "Alberto Fernández") awarding Milei the presidential scepter during the [inauguration on 10 December 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Javier_Milei "Inauguration of Javier Milei")[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Retrato_oficial_del_Presidente_Javier_%28cropped%29.jpeg/250px-Retrato_oficial_del_Presidente_Javier_%28cropped%29.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Retrato_oficial_del_Presidente_Javier_(cropped).jpeg) Milei's official portrait, 2023

Milei took office as president on 10 December 2023, amidst a lack of support in Congress,[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-130) an annual inflation rate approaching 200%,[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-131) rising poverty, and a polarized population as challenges for his presidency.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-132) His foreign minister, [Diana Mondino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana_Mondino "Diana Mondino"), announced that Argentina would not join the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS") bloc of developing economies, as had been planned for 2024.[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-133) In his first speech as president, Milei warned of an [economic shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_(economics) "Shock (economics)"), described as [shock therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_therapy_(economics) "Shock therapy (economics)") in monetary terms, to be used to fix Argentina's financial woes.[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-134)[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-135)[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-136) Following his inauguration, Milei saw his popularity rise. After the first governmental and economic reforms he and his ministers made, 53% of the Argentine people had a good, or excellent, opinion of him, according to a poll by Aresco on 15 December.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-137)

Milei's cabinet includes ministers from [La Libertad Avanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Libertad_Avanza "La Libertad Avanza") and [Juntos por el Cambio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juntos_por_el_Cambio "Juntos por el Cambio").[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-138)[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-139)[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-140) In his first acts as president, Milei signed 13 decrees related to his cabinet members. He also lowered the number of ministries from 18 to 9. He appointed three secretariats with portfolio rank, including his sister, to the position of General Secretary of the Presidency, after modifying the anti- [nepotism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepotism "Nepotism") law prohibiting the appointment of family members.[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-141)[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-142)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Zelensky_en_la_Casa_Rosada_3.jpg/250px-Zelensky_en_la_Casa_Rosada_3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Zelensky_en_la_Casa_Rosada_3.jpg) Milei and Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") in Buenos Aires, 10 December 2023

Milei signed [Decree 70/2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decree_70/2023 "Decree 70/2023"), deregulating the Argentine economy. Since such a move is subject to approval by Congress, it called for an extraordinary session, to be held in the first months of 2024.\[ _[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\][\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-143) As part of the measure, an estimated 5,000 public sector employees were expected to be affected.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-144) An Argentine court halted the labor reforms that were part of the decree.[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-145) The CGT also organized a [general strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decree_70/2023#Reactions "Decree 70/2023") against the proposed policies involving tens of thousands of Argentine workers on a 12-hour strike, which resulted in the cancellation of hundreds of flights.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-146)[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-147)

In March 2024, when Argentina submitted a complaint against Venezuela to the [International Civil Aviation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Civil_Aviation_Organization "International Civil Aviation Organization") for violating the [Convention on International Civil Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_International_Civil_Aviation "Convention on International Civil Aviation"), Milei and Venezuelan President [Nicolás Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro "Nicolás Maduro") traded accusations.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-148) That month, Milei approved the transfer of two military [Mi-171E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mil_Mi-17 "Mil Mi-17") helicopters to Ukraine, which it later received.[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-149) On 18 April, Argentina formally submitted a request to become a [NATO global partner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO_global_partners "NATO global partners").[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-150) Meanwhile, [various anti-government protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Argentina_protests "2024 Argentina protests") took place from January to June over e.g. not increasing university funds and cuts to subsidies and social programmes.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:1-151) These protests occurred in the context of broad, general approval according to a poll published by [Clarín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clar%C3%ADn_(Argentine_newspaper) "Clarín (Argentine newspaper)") on 7 June 2024, where most Argentines had a positive image of their president and an optimistic view of future economic measures.[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-152) Under his watch, Argentina exited deficit for the first time in 123 years.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-153) In a speech given to the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Economic_Forum "World Economic Forum"), Milei encouraged a worldwide push for libertarianism, claiming Argentina's was a successful example. He also stated that "radical feminism" was a "distortion" of equality.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-154)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/VdL_Mercosur_2024.jpg/250px-VdL_Mercosur_2024.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:VdL_Mercosur_2024.jpg) Milei and other [Mercosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercosur "Mercosur") leaders with [European Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Commission "European Commission") President [Ursula von der Leyen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen "Ursula von der Leyen") at the 65th Mercosur Summit in [Montevideo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montevideo "Montevideo"), Uruguay, 5 December 2024

In 2024, legal scholar [Manuel García-Mansilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Garc%C3%ADa-Mansilla "Manuel García-Mansilla") was nominated to fill the vacancy left by Minister [Juan Carlos Maqueda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Carlos_Maqueda "Juan Carlos Maqueda") and Ariel Lijo was nominated to fill the vacancy left by [Elena Highton de Nolasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elena_Highton_de_Nolasco "Elena Highton de Nolasco") to the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Argentina "Supreme Court of Argentina") by Milei. On 26 February 2025, Milei appointed both of them by presidential decrees after they failed to obtain clearance in the senate. Some lawmakers questioned Lijo's fitness to serve on the court over corruption concerns, and [Human Rights Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch "Human Rights Watch") called Milei's appointments "one of the most serious attacks against the independence of the Supreme Court in Argentina since the return of democracy".[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-155)[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-156) On 4 April 2025 both were rejected by the Senate, with Mansilla receiving 51 votes against and 21 in favor and Lijo receiving 43 votes against and 27 in favour,[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-:22-157) in what was branded as a tactical alliance between [UP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Homeland "Union for the Homeland") and [PRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Proposal "Republican Proposal"). The primary reason was the majority of senators disapproval of appointment by decree to the Supreme Court which was described as a political defeat for Milei.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-158)

The government has adopted a policy of scaling back its involvement in [public health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health "Public health") and has cut budgets for perinatal care. [Infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality "Infant mortality") rose by 6.25% in 2024, breaking a long-standing downward trend.[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-159)

In May 2025, Milei signed a decree to tighten [immigration laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Argentina "Immigration to Argentina") in Argentina.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-160) Milei said that immigrants were bringing "chaos and abuse" to Argentina, and ordered that the path to citizenship be restricted, with foreign nationals having to reside two years without interruption in Argentina and show proof of income or "sufficient means", plus a clean criminal record in their home countries. Presidential spokesman [Manuel Adorni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Adorni "Manuel Adorni") said that Milei's decree "honors history to Make Argentina Great Again", as the opposition likened Milei's move to Trump's similar immigration policies in the US.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-161)

On 18 May 2025, local elections were held in the capital of Argentina, Buenos Aires. Manuel Adorni, Milei's top presidential spokesman, attained victory with 30% of the votes, a larger margin than anticipated; while the center right opposition, PRO, suffered its worst defeat since taking power in Buenos Aires two decades ago, with only 16%.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-162) The peronist candidate Leandro Santoro lost with 27% of the votes. The voting turnout for the election was at a historic low of 53% despite compulsory voting.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-163)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Milei_y_Le%C3%B3n_XIV.jpg/250px-Milei_y_Le%C3%B3n_XIV.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Milei_y_Le%C3%B3n_XIV.jpg) Milei with Pope [Leo XIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_XIV "Leo XIV") in June 2025

In August 2025, Karina Milei, who holds the post of Secretary General of the Presidency and is a close adviser and sibling to President Javier Milei, had been implicated in a corruption scandal centering on alleged kickbacks from pharmaceutical contracts managed through Argentina's National Disability Agency (ANDIS). The allegations emerged after leaked audio recordings from Diego Spagnuolo, the former head of ANDIS and previously a lawyer for Milei, in which he claims that companies seeking disability-related contracts were pressured to pay an "8% commission" (kickback) on contracts, a portion of which would go to Karina Milei and her associate Eduardo "Lule" Menem.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-164) The scandal has been politically damaging as it contradicts Milei's public image as an outsider committed to fighting corruption; it has provoked public outrage, especially given that the alleged misconduct involves contracts aimed at vulnerable populations; and it may have electoral consequences for his party (La Libertad Avanza) in forthcoming legislative elections.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-165) Though there is no evidence that Javier Milei had a role in the scandal, it still affected his chances of winning the electoral vote in Buenos Aires, resulting in a Peronist victory.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto10-166)

On 7 September 2025, following weeks after Karina's scandal came to light, Milei's party had lost a key electoral election in Buenos Aires, with La Libertad Avanza attaining 33% of the votes while the Peronist opposition received 47%. President Milei conceded defeat and reflected on the reasons for the loss, but vowed to not repeat his mistakes and to accelerate his economic agenda ahead of schedule.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto10-166)[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-167) In 2023, Peronism got 4.3million votes (about 45%) while LLA received 2.3million. This time, it got 3.8million votes while LLA attained 2.7million votes. Voter turnout was only 61%.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-168)

On 10 September 2025, Milei reorganized the [Secretariat of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretariat_of_the_Interior_(Argentina) "Secretariat of the Interior (Argentina)") into a ministry, reversing a downgrade in 2024 that he had made as part of his pledge to reduce public deficits and the size of the government. This occurred amid La Libertad Avanza losing heavily in the [2025 Buenos Aires provincial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Buenos_Aires_provincial_election "2025 Buenos Aires provincial election").[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-169) In October 2025, President Javier Milei's party, La Libertad Avanza, won a landslide victory in [midterm elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Argentine_legislative_election "2025 Argentine legislative election"), making it easier for Milei to push ahead with his programme of radical spending cuts and free-market reforms.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-170)

In late 2025, the Milei administration announced the creation of a new "Agencia Nacional de Migraciones" (National Immigration Agency), moving control from the Interior Ministry to the Security Ministry, with a focus on border control and security, described by some as an Argentine equivalent to U.S. [Immigration and Customs Enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_and_Customs_Enforcement "Immigration and Customs Enforcement") (ICE).[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-171)

### Later actions (2025–present)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Speaking_at_CPAC_Budapest_2026_-.jpg/250px-Speaking_at_CPAC_Budapest_2026_-.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Speaking_at_CPAC_Budapest_2026_-.jpg) Speaking at CPAC Budapest 2026

Javier Milei, following a strong showing in the recent mid-term elections where he tripled his presence in Congress, has embarked on a drive to solidify his reform agenda in Argentina. First, he convened governors and the Buenos Aires mayor at the Casa Rosada, gathering 20 out of 23 provinces (while excluding four Peronist leaders) to build consensus around his 2026 Budget and structural reforms focused on labour, tax and penal-code changes. Concurrently, he announced a sweeping “new economic roadmap” to be deployed once the new Congress seats in December, featuring major labour, tax and pension overhauls aimed at restoring fiscal balance, formalizing informal work, simplifying the tax burden and protecting private property — part of his broader vision of making Argentina “the freest country in the world.”[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-172)[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-173)

In February 2026, the National Congress approved legislation supported by Milei to liberalise Argentina's labour laws.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-174)[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-175)

### Economy

Main article: [Economic reforms of Javier Milei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_reforms_of_Javier_Milei "Economic reforms of Javier Milei")

See also: [Shock therapy (economics)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_therapy_(economics) "Shock therapy (economics)") and [Economy of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Argentina "Economy of Argentina")

In 2024, under President Javier Milei, Argentina saw major economic changes, focusing on [austerity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austerity "Austerity"), [deregulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deregulation "Deregulation"), and reducing government spending. The most pressing issue for Milei was hyperinflation. Inflation dropped from 300% in May 2024 to 55.9% in March 2025, the lowest level in over three years, with monthly inflation hitting a five-year low of 2.2% in January.[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-reason.com-176)[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-indec.gob.ar-177)[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-reuters.com-178) Milei cut government positions by approximately 52,000, a reduction of between 9.6% and 15.7%; reduced salaries of high-ranking officials; and shut down the tax agency (AFIP), replacing it with a smaller one, saving billions.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Milei_shuts_down_Argentina's_tax_ag-179)[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-bloomberglinea.com-180)[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Hall-181)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto7-182)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto6-183) While poverty rose to 57% early in 2024, it declined to 38.1% by Q3, with homelessness also decreasing.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-ReferenceA-184)[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-En_el_tercer_trimestre_la_pobreza_s-185)

The economy shrank in early 2024, but grew 3.9% in Q3, helped by an 80.2% rebound in agriculture. The Argentine _peso_ strengthened by 44.2% against the U.S. dollar, boosting average wages in dollar terms to $990.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto5-186) Wages, along with purchasing power in Argentina began falling in October 2023, before Milei was elected and inaugurated as president. By the start of his presidency, real wages fell by more than 20%.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Labor_Monitor-187)[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-batimes.com.ar-188) They have mostly rebounded back to their November 2023 levels, ending the first year of his presidency as an economic success.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-milei.ufm.edu-189)[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-laderechadiario.com-190)[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Labor_Monitor-187)[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-riotimesonline.com-191)

Argentina paid off $4.3billion in debt, and investor confidence soared, pushing bond prices higher. According to Gallup, the confidence in the economy by everyday people soared in end of 2024, reaching heights not seen since 2015.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Vigers-192) The country signed a free trade deal with the EU, and credit ratings improved. Forecasts predict economic growth of 3.5%–5.5% in 2025 as inflation stabilizes and investments increase.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto2-193)[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-GDP_rebounds_in_the_third_quarter-194)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto4-195)[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-americaeconomia.com-196)[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto-197)[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-ReferenceB-198)[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto5-186) Argentina's market risk assessment fell below 500 points for the first time since 2018.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Argentina_investors_bet_on_Milei's-199)[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Argentina's_risk_index_rebounds_aft-200)

2025 was a turbulent year for Milei. Inflation had become 1.5% a month, and poverty had markedly fallen to 31.6%, the lowest it had been since 2018. In the first half of 2025, reports indicate that the middle class grew by 7.7million, rebounding back to pre-pandemic levels of 39%[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-201)[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto12-202)[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-bloomberg.com-203) Moody's have increased Argentina's rating from 'Ca' to 'Caa3 as Argentina's economy demonstrates solid recovery progress. While average wages increased 20.7% in the first half of 2025, outpacing the country's 15.1% inflation rate, and GDP was expected to grow 5.5%,[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-riotimesonline-204) final GDP for the year ended up at 4.37%,[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-205) and while wages rose 28.8%, the inflation rate reached 31.8%, resulting in a 2.5% decline in real purchasing power.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-206)[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-207) Milei contended that a fiscal surplus remained non-negotiable, with a target of 1.6% for 2025,[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-208) and a surplus of 1.4% was achieved.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-209)

Karina's scandal and the subsequent loss of the legislative elections in Buenos Aires resulted in backlash from the markets, temporarily jeopardizing the macro-economic situation that Milei had dedicated his presidency to date, as the market feared that Milei losing the midterm elections would result in the stifling of his agenda.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto10-166) The meltdown caused Argentina to blow through 1billion dollars of reserves to uphold the peso. U.S. Secretary of the Treasury Scott Bessent's announcement that all options are available between the American treasury and Argentine government halted the gradual implosion.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto11-210)[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto13-211) The economic reforms and policies have had a significant impact on the average Argentine, as some common expenses such as private school tuition and health insurance plans have risen above CPI.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-212)

Despite soaring disapproval ratings, Milei achieved victory in the legislative elections of 2025, attaining a larger than expected share of votes. The victory galvanized the economy, leading towards bonds increasing by 23% in a day, the Argentine stock market growing by 70% compared to last month and the peso briefly appreciating in value. The country risk index dropped by 40% after the elections.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-213)[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto14-214)[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto15-215)

President Milei stopped an economic crises from unraveling even further in 2024, but recovery has proven difficult and in some cases uneven. “Without investment, amid business closures and no formal job creation, the Argentine economy is moving neither backward nor forward,” said Lorenzo Sigaut Gravina, an economist at consulting firm Equilibra, which forecasts just two percent growth this year.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-216)

#### Inflation

Inflation was the foremost largest issue dominating contemporary Argentine politics, as it spiraled out of control during and for some time after the elections of 2023.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-217) The issue had been a consistent phenomenon throughout the history of Argentina, a country whose economy had been mismanaged for decades.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto9-218) Milei, convinced that the Peronist establishment largely caused the economic crisis, made it his main objective during the first and second year of his presidency to stop the situation from getting worse through anti-inflationary economic policies, and to diminish the influence of Peronism.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-219)[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-220) Annual inflation stood at 254.2% in the first month of Milei's term,[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-221)[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-222) and reached a peak of 300% in May 2024.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-bloomberg.com-203) Monthly inflation slowed in 2024 as Milei continued to push austerity and deregulation measures to revive the country's struggling economy.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-223) Annual inflation rate came in at 39.4% in July 2025.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-224) On the same month, monthly inflation reached a 5-year low of 1.5%.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-bloomberg.com-203)

Wages, along with purchasing power in Argentina began falling in October 2023, before Milei was elected and inaugurated as president. By the start of his presidency, real wages fell by more than 20%.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Labor_Monitor-187)[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-batimes.com.ar-188) In 2024, wages rose by 145.5%, outpacing the 117.8% inflation rate for the first time since 2021, according to INDEC. According to Nowcast, the year-over-year increase in total family income (ITF) reached 185.7%. In October 2024, CEPA reported that private sector wages had nearly returned to November 2023 levels. However, public sector incomes remained 14.8% lower, and informal workers were still down 21.3%.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-laderechadiario.com-190)[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-Labor_Monitor-187)[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-riotimesonline.com-191) Continuing this trend, wages have risen by 20.7% in the first half of 2025, above an inflation rate of 15.1%, however it again fell below inflation in the second half of 2025 for some sectors.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-riotimesonline-204)[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-225)[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-226)[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-227)[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-228)

The defeat in the local elections in Buenos Aires scared the market, with investors afraid that Milei would not be able to maintain his free market agenda in his presidential term.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto11-210) Victory in the legislative elections caused the Peso to rally temporarily before stabilizing again.[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto14-214)

#### Reforms

During his campaign and throughout his presidency, Javier Milei has said several times that the reforms he plans to enact are "painful", but "necessary", contending that it is "the only way". "There is no alternative to a shock adjustment", Milei said during his inauguration. "There is no money."[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-229) In June 2024, Milei was able to pass his tax and 'omnibus' bill. The bill, watered down significantly from its original form, aimed at deregulating and reforming several sectors and state owned enterprises in Argentina, also included reforms concerning labour, commerce and real estate and various social programmes such as healthcare.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-230)

President Javier Milei achieved a budget surplus within the first few months in office by gutting chunks of the government structure and downsizing it drastically and reducing the salaries of high ranking authorities, leading to a reduction of government spending by 30%. This has resulted in a financial surplus for 6 consecutive months for the first time since 2008.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-231) The reforms have led to 9.6% of all federal employees (approximately 52,000) being laid off, saving the government nearly 2.1billion in taxes.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto7-182)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto6-183)

In July 2024, the Argentine National Congress approved Milei's Ley de Bases, a wide-ranging reform law that marked a major legislative achievement for a government with limited parliamentary representation; a central element of the law is the Incentive Regime for Large Investments (Régimen de Incentivos para Grandes Inversiones, RIGI), which seeks to attract large-scale domestic and foreign investment by guaranteeing stable tax, foreign exchange, trade, and capital repatriation rules for up to 30 years. The regime applies to projects exceeding US$200 million in sectors such as energy, oil and gas, mining, infrastructure, forestry, tourism, technology, and steel, with enhanced benefits for investments above US$1 billion, including lower corporate and dividend taxes, phased elimination of export taxes, and exemptions from capital and trade controls. RIGI is predicted to reduce investor exposure to Argentina's historically volatile economic conditions and support a shift away from a state-centered, inflation-prone economic model toward greater financial stability and private investment.[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-232)

Milei's repealing of rent controls in December 2023 has caused rental unit supply to increase by 190%. This resulted in a 40% decline in real price of rental properties when adjusted for inflation.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-233)[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-234)[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-235) Inflation-adjusted rents have remained relatively stable since. From January to May 2025 rents have increased by 1.6% after adjusting for inflation.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-236)[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-237)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Real_rents_in_the_City_of_Buenos_Aires_%282016_pesos%29.svg/250px-Real_rents_in_the_City_of_Buenos_Aires_%282016_pesos%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Real_rents_in_the_City_of_Buenos_Aires_(2016_pesos).svg) Real (inflation-adjusted) rents declined by around 40% after rent control was abolished, using data from Zonaprop and INDEC

"He has done more good than many people expected", said Alejandro Werner, a former official with the International Monetary Fund. "Maybe I would choose to do it a different way. But, sometimes, to change things, you need somebody that’s a little bit of a fanatic to really move the needle. And he has done it." Though the macroeconomic side had become stabilized, the societal consequences were palpable. Patience towards president Milei's reforms runs thin as his economic reforms cuts into salaries and pushes up the cost of living.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-auto9-218)

Data from the IARAF institute shows President Milei decreased government spending by approximately 8 billion dollars in 2 years.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-238)

Milei's government initiated a program titled "Fiscal Innocence" in a bid to integrate an estimated $170 billion dollars that reside outside the formal financial system and drive growth. That program would allow Argentinians to deposit money into the banks without needing to declare a source of income or incur additional tax obligations. This program has met with little success however to shift the trends resulting from the 2001 forced conversion to the peso which saw most Argentines lose nearly 75% of their savings. Longstanding distrust of the government and financial systems following this and other incidents has led to wide spread retention of hard currency, termed "mattress dollars." Argentines continue to buy around $2 billion of foreign currency every month as a hedge against inflation.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-239)

#### Poverty

During the first six months of his presidency, poverty rates increased by over 11 percentage points, from 41.7% to 52.9%, briefly peaking at 57.4% at the end of January 2024.[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-240) In response, his government pointed out that poverty rates were already rising under previous administrations. According to INDEC, poverty rates fell to 38.1% during the third quarter of 2024.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-ReferenceA-184)[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-En_el_tercer_trimestre_la_pobreza_s-185) Conversely, the Catholic University of Argentina (UCA) estimated poverty for the same quarter as 41.6%, adjusted for the reduced consumption rates as well as rising healthcare costs.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-241)[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-242)

The Ministry of Human Capital estimated a drop in poverty during the first quarter of 2025 from 38.1% to 31.7%. An economist working for CEDLAS (Center for distributive, Labour and Social Studies), estimates that between October 2024 and March 2025, the poverty rate dropped to 34.4%.[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-243) A report published in July 2025 largely confirmed these predictions, finding that Argentina's urban poverty rate dropped to 31.6% in the first half of 2025, its lowest level since 2018. Extreme poverty also decreased from 18.2% to 7.4% annually.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-244)

INDEC reported the official poverty rate to be 31.6% of the general population in the first half of 2025, with household poverty coming in at 24.1%. The UCA disputes the magnitude of the reduction, arguing that INDEC uses an inadequate methodology.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-245)[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-246) They find that poverty has decreased to 36.3%, compared to their previous estimate of 45.6% in the same period a year ago, lowest since 2018.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-247)

Income inequality increased slightly from 0.417 to 0.436 in 2024, but decreased to 0.427 by the end of 2025.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-248)[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-249)

#### International trade and payments

In June, during a meeting in Berlin, Milei and German chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz "Olaf Scholz") expressed support for a [free trade agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU%E2%80%93Mercosur_Association_Agreement "EU–Mercosur Association Agreement") between the EU and [Mercosur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercosur "Mercosur").[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-250) An agreement on the free trade deal was announced on 6 December 2024.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei#cite_note-251)
